# Rack Power Cap Standard, revision 7 (in force)

This standard is the only authority for compiling rack power caps. Where any export, note, mailbox, draft or
earlier compile disagrees with it, this standard wins. Notes that start with "legacy compile:" were written by
the retired cap compiler, whose figures are known to be wrong; they are kept for history and carry no authority.

## 1. Sources and the record layout

Every source file carries one record per data line with eight fields in this order: id, kind, subject, at,
value, unit, attrs, note. Each source names these fields with its own header words. attrs holds key=value
pairs separated by semicolons; keys are case-insensitive and numbers in attrs follow section 5. Record ids are
case-insensitive, and a subject that names a record may carry the prefix rec:, which is not part of the id.
Record kinds:

- RD telemetry reading. attrs: src (pdu, bmc or agent), dom (AC or DC), batch, outlet (pdu only).
- HV host inventory revision. value is the revision number. attrs: rack, model, psu, n (PSU count), tenant,
  status (active or retired), serial.
- CV circuit revision (value revision; attrs rack, feed, volts, amps). MV server model revision (value
  revision; attrs va, pf in per mille, idle in W, domain AC or DC). PR PSU revision (value revision; attrs rated
  in W). QV placement policy revision for a host (value revision; attrs prio gold, silver or bronze, floor in W).
  SP site parameter revision: subject site:parameter, value revision, attrs parameter=value. The parameters are
  cont, derate, warn, crit, pool and margin in per mille, volts, step in W, tariff in milli-cents per kWh, pue in
  thousandths and corr in milliseconds; each parameter has its own revisions. TN tenant
  entitlement revision (subject tenant@site, value revision, attrs watts).
- PV PSU efficiency point (Appendix A). subject PSU model, attrs load in per mille of rated output, value the
  efficiency at that load; unit says how it is written: % (percent), frac (fraction of one), bp (basis points),
  permille, or the percent sign written inside the value.
- AL alias: the subject name means attrs target from time at (inclusive) until attrs until (exclusive). A
  target may itself be an alias, resolved at the same instant.
- VO void of the record whose id is the subject. ER erratum: the reading whose id is the subject is replaced by
  value, in W. CA outlet calibration from time at: attrs gain in parts per million and offset in mW. PF export
  profile: every reading of the batch named by subject was exported multiplied by 1000/scale, so it is read
  back by multiplying by scale/1000. RT ratification of the record whose id is the subject, held in attrs file.
- AM amendment of a host or circuit: attrs field (floor_w, cap_ceiling_w, priority or breaker_a) and value.

## 2. Which records are in force

The files whose names say re-export, replay, backfill, legacy, prev, draft, rescan or prelim are superseded or
unapproved. A record inside one of them is in force only when an in-force RT record outside those files names
its id and its file. Every other file is in force. A record is voided when an in-force VO names its id; VO and RT
records cannot be voided. A record id that appears on more than one line is one record (a replay), whatever
file it sits in.

For HV, CV, MV, PR, QV, SP and TN the effective record of a subject is the in-force record with the highest
revision number among those timed before the freeze; on a tie the later time wins, then the greater id.
Revision numbers decide, never the order of lines or files.

## 3. Freeze and window

The as-of freeze is 2026-03-30T00:00:00Z and the telemetry window runs from 2026-03-23T00:00:00Z up to, but not
including, the freeze. A record timed at or after the freeze is post-cut. Post-cut amendments are carried
forward and are applied only in the final cap compile; every other post-cut record is ignored. Readings timed
before the window are ignored.

## 4. Times

A time is one of: ISO 8601 with Z or with a numeric offset; a date and time with no offset, which is the local
civil time of the site its record belongs to; epoch seconds (10 digits) or epoch milliseconds (13 digits); a
spreadsheet serial day number counted from 1899-12-30 00:00 UTC, rounded to the nearest second; the compact
form YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ; or the RFC 2822 form. The site of a record is the site of its subject: for a host, the
first four characters of its effective rack; for a circuit or an outlet, the first four characters of the id;
for tenant entitlements and site parameters, the named site; for VO, ER and RT, the site of the record named.
Records with no site never carry a time without an offset.
Site clocks: fra1 is UTC+1, and UTC+2 from the last Sunday of March 01:00 UTC to the last Sunday of October
01:00 UTC. iad2 is UTC-5 and den1 is UTC-7, each one hour ahead of that from the second Sunday of March 02:00
local time to the first Sunday of November 02:00 local time. sin1 is UTC+8 all year. Local times never fall
inside a clock change hour.

## 5. Numbers and missing values

The decimal separator is the last of "." and "," in the number when both appear; when only one of them appears
once it is the decimal separator; when one of them appears more than once it is grouping. Spaces, the narrow
no-break space and apostrophes are always grouping. An exponent may follow e or E. A value that is empty, "-",
"n/a", "NULL" or "NaN" is missing; a missing reading is not a sample. Zero is a real value.

## 6. Hosts

The canonical host id is n followed by five digits, lower case. Upper case and the suffix .pwr.corp name the
same host. A serial number (HV attrs serial) names the host it belongs to. Every other host name, asset tags
included, resolves through AL records at the time of the record that uses it. A host is in scope when its
effective HV record says active. Its site is the first four characters of its effective rack. Nameplate W is the
model VA times pf divided by 1000, rounded half to even. Floor W is the larger of the model idle W and the
effective policy floor; a host with no policy is bronze with floor 0. Amendments timed before the freeze are
applied in time order, then id order: floor_w sets the floor, cap_ceiling_w sets the cap ceiling, priority sets
the priority and breaker_a sets the circuit breaker.

## 7. Circuits

Usable W of a circuit is volts times breaker amps times cont times derate divided by 1000000, rounded down,
using the effective SP record of its site.

## 8. Readings

A reading becomes AC milliwatts in this order. Multiply the written number by its unit factor (W 1000, kW
1000000, mW 1, VA 1000, kVA 1000000, BTU/h 293.071; unit words are case-insensitive and watt means W, Btu/hr
means BTU/h) and, when the batch has an in-force PF record, by scale/1000; round half to even once. A VA reading
is then multiplied by the effective model pf of the host and divided by 1000, rounded half to even. A pdu
reading is then corrected by the latest in-force CA record of its outlet timed at or before the reading (when
two such records share that latest time, the greater id wins):
multiply by (1000000 + gain)/1000000, round half to even, add offset. A DC reading is then turned into AC: the
load in per mille is the reading divided by the host PSU count times rated W (both in W), rounded half to even
and held between 1 and 1000; divide by the Appendix A efficiency at that load and multiply by 10000 (efficiency
in basis points), rounding half to even. An in-force ER record naming the reading replaces the result.

For one host and one reading time, a pdu reading beats a bmc reading, which beats an agent reading. A missing
reading takes no part in that choice, so the best reading of that time that has a value is the one kept.

## 9. Demand

For each in-scope host, over the window: samples is the count of reading times kept; mean, the 95th percentile
by nearest rank and the peak are in W with one decimal; energy is in Wh, each reading standing for 15 minutes.
All rounding is half to even. A host with no samples has no mean, percentile, peak or energy.
